Q1. A job would be completed in 6 days of work. Due to a 1 day stoppage, in how many calendar days is it completed?
A. 7 days
B. 5 3/5 days
C. 4 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 7 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
T_{calendar}=T_{active}+T_{idle}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
T_{active}=\frac{114}{4+15}
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+1=7
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 7 days

---

Q2. 12 workers were assigned to a job. 4 workers left after 8 days. How many days late will the job finish?
A. 10 1/2 days
B. 6 4/5 days
C. 8 1/2 days
D. 14 1/2 days
Answer: 8 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_worker_removed_delay
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
D=T_{actual}-T_{planned}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
R=300-12\times 8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
D=\frac{204}{8}-\frac{204}{12}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=T_a-T_p
\]
Answer: 8 1/2 days

---

Q3. In a printing job, three workers complete 12, 15 and 18 pages per hour. How many pages are completed in 5 hours?
A. 180 pages
B. 227 pages
C. 225 pages
D. 231 pages
Answer: 225 pages
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_typist_pages_per_hour
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 5+15\times 5+18\times 5
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=225
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 225 pages

---

Q4. A tank is already one-fourth full. A pipe can fill the whole tank in 12 hours. In how many hours will the remaining part be filled?
A. 12 hours
B. 14 hours
C. 9 hours
D. 10 4/5 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_partial_tank_initially_filled
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{1-f}{r_f}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=(1-f)F
\]
Answer: 9 hours

---

Q5. The fraction \(\frac{4}{9}\) of a job is completed in 12 days. How many days will the whole job take?
A. 108 days
B. 21 days
C. 5 1/3 days
D. 27 days
Answer: 27 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_partial_completion_then_team
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=12\times \frac{9}{4}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=27
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=12\div \frac{4}{9}=27
\]
Answer: 27 days

---

Q6. Water flows into a tank at 25 litres per minute. In 20 minutes, how many litres will enter the tank?
A. 506 litres
B. 400 litres
C. 502 litres
D. 500 litres
Answer: 500 litres
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_capacity_leakage_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
C=rt
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
C=25\times 20
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
C=500
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
C=r\times t
\]
Answer: 500 litres

Q7. A factory uses three machines. Machine A makes 12 parts per hour, B makes 15, and C makes 18. How many parts are produced in 6 hours?
A. 267 items
B. 270 items

Answer: 270 items
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_parallel_machine_batches
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 6+15\times 6+18\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=270
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 270 items

Q8. Pipe A fills a tank in 12 hours. After 2 hours, a leak begins which empties a full tank in 30 hours. Find the total time to fill the tank?
A. 18 2/3 hours
B. 22 2/5 hours
C. 15 2/3 hours
D. 14 2/3 hours
Answer: 18 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_drain_after_partial_fill
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/30
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 2/3 hours

---

Q9. 80 of 300 forms are complete. 10 workers are available and 12 days remain. How many additional workers are required?
A. 4 workers
B. 6 workers
C. 11.25 workers
D. 9 workers
Answer: 9 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{220}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 9 workers

---

Q10. A works first and B works next, then the order repeats. A completes 10 forms and B completes 4 forms on their turns. How many days are needed for 90 forms?
A. 15.12 days
B. 8 3/5 days
C. 12 3/5 days
D. 9 3/5 days
Answer: 12 3/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_days_two_workers
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12.6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 3/5 days

---

Q11. 6 men and 8 women work together. A man packs 4 boxes per day and a woman packs 3 boxes per day. How many days are needed for 240 boxes?
A. 1 day
B. 5 days
C. 7 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_group_conversion_and_or
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=\frac{240}{6\times 4+8\times 3}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 5 days

---

Q12. A work requires 16 workers for 12 days with 6 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 18 days with 8 hours per day?
A. 16 workers
B. 11 workers
C. 24 workers
D. 8 workers
Answer: 8 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
16\times 12\times 6=M_2\times 18\times 8
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
M_2=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{16\times 12\times 6}{18\times 8}=8
\]
Answer: 8 workers

---

r=1/12-1/30

Q14. A worker completes 15 forms daily for 5 days and then 12 forms daily. How many days are needed for 180 forms?
A. 11 3/4 days
B. 16 1/2 days
C. 18 3/4 days
D. 13 3/4 days
Answer: 13 3/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_productivity_decay
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-r_1t_1}{r_2}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=5+\frac{180-15\times 5}{12}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=13.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+t_2
\]
Answer: 13 3/4 days

Q15. A, B and C complete a job and receive ₹7200. Their work shares are in the ratio 30:30:12. What is C's share?
A. ₹1800
B. ₹1196
C. ₹1200
D. ₹1201
Answer: ₹1200
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_wage_with_helper
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use contribution ratio for the share.
\[
Share=\frac{12}{72}\times 7200
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=1200
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹1200

---

Q16. A and B start the same job. A alone takes 12 days and B alone takes 24 days. After 3 days only B continues. How many days are needed in all?
A. 27 days
B. 14 days
C. 19 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(10+5)\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{75}{5}=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 18 days

---

Q17. Given the efficiency ratios A:B = 2:3 and B:C = 7:8, what is the ratio A:B:C?
A. 2:7:8
B. 14:21:24
C. 24:21:14
D. 2:3:8
Answer: 14:21:24
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_chain
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
B_c
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
LCM(3,7)=21
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
A:B:C=14:21:24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=14:21:24
\]
Answer: 14:21:24

Q20. In a joint job, A alone needs 8 days and B alone needs 14 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. How many days does the job take?
A. 8.95 days
B. 10.45 days
C. 7.45 days
D. 12.45 days
Answer: 7.45 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
7(T-2)+4(T-3)=56
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=7.45
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 7.45 days

---

Q21. If wages for equal time are ₹450 and ₹600 for A and B, what is their work ratio?
A. 600:450
B. 45:30
C. 3:4
D. 1050:600
Answer: 3:4
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_efficiency_from_wages
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
W_A:W_B=E_A:E_B
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
E_A:E_B=450:600
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
E_A:E_B=3:4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:4
\]
Answer: 3:4

---

Q22. The three workers A, B and C complete a work in 10 days. If A and B together complete it in 20 days, how many days will C alone need?
A. 45 days
B. 43 days
C. 48 days
D. 40 days
Answer: 40 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_unknown_worker_from_team
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}-\frac{1}{40}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_X=40
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 40 days

Q24. A tap fills 12 litres in one hour and a drain removes 5 litres in the next hour. This pattern repeats. If the tank capacity is 60 litres, in how many hours will it be full?
A. 20.42 hours
B. 16 1/3 hours
C. 17 1/3 hours
D. 22 1/3 hours
Answer: 16 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_alternating_spill_cycle
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

Q28. For the same work, AB finishes in 10 days, BC in 12 days and CA in 15 days. In how many days can A alone finish it?
A. 28 4/5 days
B. 23 days
C. 20 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_pairwise_ab_bc_ac
Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
2(A+B+C)=(A+B)+(B+C)+(C+A)
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
AB=10,\ BC=12,\ CA=15
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T_A=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A=\frac{AB+AC-BC}{2}
\]
Answer: 24 days

---

Q32. A team completes 10 files per day for 4 days and then 9 files per day after a team change. How many days are required for 72 files?
A. 6.56 days
B. 12.56 days
C. 7.56 days
D. 5 2/3 days
Answer: 7.56 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_replacement_worker
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
r_1t_1+r_2t_2=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=10\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{32}{9}=7.56
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 7.56 days

Q33. There is enough food for 24 people for 36 days. What is its duration for 40 people?
A. 20 3/5 days
B. 17 3/5 days
C. 25.92 days
D. 21 3/5 days
Answer: 21 3/5 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
24\times 36=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=21.6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{24\times 36}{40}
\]
Answer: 21 3/5 days

Q34. A tank is filled by a pipe taking 12 hours and emptied by another pipe taking 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be filled if both are opened?
A. 14.14 hours
B. 25.71 hours
C. 17.14 hours
D. 21.14 hours
Answer: 17.14 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=17.14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 17.14 hours

Q36. Two filling pipes A and B take 12 and 18 hours respectively. If pipe A is closed after 2 hours, how many hours are needed to fill the tank?
A. 11 hours
B. 18 hours
C. 17 hours
D. 15 hours
Answer: 15 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_pipe_closed_before_completion
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=(r_A+r_B)t_1,\quad T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+2)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{26}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Answer: 15 hours

\[

\]

Q38. There are 720 ration packets in a camp. If 36 packets are used each day, how many days will they last?
A. 24 days
B. 17 days
C. 20 days
D. 30 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_resource_rate_equivalence
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720}{36}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Answer: 20 days

Q51. 12 workers working 8 hours per day finish the job in 10 days. If the daily working time is 6 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 16 days?
A. 19 workers
B. 13 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 12 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]

\[

\]

\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q52. A and B finish a work together in 8 days, and their efficiency ratio is 5:7. How many days will A alone need?

Q53. A pipe fills a tank in 12 hours. A leak slows the filling to 22 hours. What time will the leak take to empty the tank alone?

\]
Answer: 26 2/5 hours

Q55. A tank is filled using pipes A and B. Pipe A alone takes 12 hours and pipe B alone takes 18 hours. If pipe B is opened 2 hours later, how long will the tank take to fill?

B. 6 hours

Answer: 8 hours

Family: pc_multiple_pipes_timing

\[

\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{30}{3+2}=8
\]

\]
Answer: 8 hours

Q56. A and B work on alternate hours, starting with A. A completes 8 files in an hour and B completes 4 files in an hour. If 72 files are to be completed, in how many hours will the job be finished?
A. 9 3/5 hours
B. 16 hours
C. 10 hours
D. 12 hours
Answer: 12 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 hours

A. 8.21 days

C. 4.97 days
D. 12.21 days
Answer: 6.21 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_replacement_worker
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
r_1t_1+r_2t_2=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=20\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{80}{19}=6.21
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 6.21 days

\]

\]

Q97. A and B work by turns. A starts and completes 6 documents per turn; B completes 4. How many hours will 60 documents take?
A. 8 hours
B. 14 2/5 hours
C. 9 hours
D. 12 hours
Answer: 12 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 hours

Q110. If A can complete a work in 12 days and B can complete it in 24 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?

\]
Answer: 8 days

W=LCM(24,15)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/24=5,\quad B=120/15=8
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{5+8}=9.23
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 15}{24+15}=9.23
\]
Answer: 9.23 days

---

Q113. A team completes 30 files per day for the first 5 days. After that, more workers join and the team completes 41 files per day. If 180 files must be completed, how many days are needed in all?
A. 1.73 days
B. 5.73 days
C. 6.73 days
D. 8 3/5 days
Answer: 5.73 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=30\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{30}{41}=5.73
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 5.73 days

Q122. A and B work by turns. A starts and completes 10 documents per turn; B completes 6. How many days will 60 documents take?
A. 5 1/2 days
B. 9 1/3 days
C. 12 1/3 days
D. 7 1/3 days
Answer: 7 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_days_two_workers
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=7\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 7 1/3 days

\]

Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_two_tanks_transfer
Explanation:

\]

\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]

Q188. A alone finishes a job in 6 days, while B alone finishes it in 10 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?
A. 16 days
B. 3 3/4 days
C. 6 days
D. 8 days
Answer: 3 3/4 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,10)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/10=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+3}=3.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 10}{6+10}=3.75
\]
Answer: 3 3/4 days

Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[

\]

\[

\]

\]

D. 61 litres

Family: pc_overflow_waste_rate

\]

\[
V_w=10\times 6
\]

\[
V_w=60
\]

\[
V_w=r\times t_e
\]
Answer: 60 litres

Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 24=25\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=19.2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 24}{25}
\]
Answer: 19 1/5 days

Q303. A repair team completes 18 metres per day and daily damage removes 4 metres. How long will 120 metres take?
A. 13.57 days
B. 5.57 days
C. 8.57 days
D. 6.43 days
Answer: 8.57 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]

\[
T=\frac{120}{18-4}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=8.57
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 8.57 days

Q310. A tank is filled by a pipe in 15 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 20 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?

B. 60 hours
C. 57 hours
D. 55 hours
Answer: 60 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=60
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 60 hours

Q316. 12 workers have done 120 files out of 360. To finish the rest in 8 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 13.5 workers
B. 13 workers
C. 18 workers
D. 19 workers
Answer: 18 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{240}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 18 workers

Q317. A begins a job alone. A can finish it in 12 days and B can finish it in 10 days. If B joins after 5 days, when will the job be completed from the start?
A. 8.18 days
B. 9.18 days
C. 6.55 days
D. 12.18 days
Answer: 8.18 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=20\times 5,\quad R=240-100
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{140}{20+24}=8.18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5+\frac{240-20\times 5}{20+24}
\]
Answer: 8.18 days

Q318. One worker can do a job in 15 days and another can do it in 12 days. How long will they take together?
A. 6 2/3 days
B. 12 days
C. 13 1/2 days
D. 27 days
Answer: 6 2/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(15,12)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/15=4,\quad B=60/12=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{4+5}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 12}{15+12}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Answer: 6 2/3 days

Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]

Q336. A job has 300 forms. 12 workers have finished 120 forms, and 10 days remain. How many more workers should be added?
A. 4.8 workers
B. 12 workers
C. 3 workers
D. 6 workers
Answer: 6 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{180}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 6 workers

Explanation:
Set up the rate relation for this case.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Put the given values into that relation.
\[
T=\frac{150}{15-3}
\]
Solve for the asked value.
\[
T=12.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 12 1/2 days

Q377. A filling pipe takes 15 hours for a tank. With a leak, the same tank fills in 21 hours. In how many hours can the leak empty a full tank?
A. 52 1/2 hours
B. 42 hours
C. 49 1/2 hours
D. 58 1/2 hours
Answer: 52 1/2 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{21}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=52.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 52 1/2 hours

Q379. Two workers take turns, with A starting. A handles 10 files per turn and B handles 5 files per turn. In how many hours will 72 files be finished?

D. 14 2/5 hours
Answer: 9 2/5 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_hours
Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i

\[
W_{cycle}=10+5
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=9.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 9 2/5 hours

Q383. 12 workers have done 96 files out of 360. To finish the rest in 10 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 22.5 workers
B. 13 workers
C. 15 workers
D. 11 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{264}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q384. A works alone for 3 days on a job. A's time is 8 days and B's time is 18 days. How many days are needed from the start if B joins afterward?
A. 1.46 days
B. 4.85 days
C. 6.46 days
D. 9.46 days
Answer: 6.46 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=45\times 3,\quad R=360-135
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{225}{45+20}=6.46
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{360-45\times 3}{45+20}
\]
Answer: 6.46 days

Q385. A takes 15 days to finish a work and B takes 20 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 17 1/2 days
B. 35 days
C. 15 days
D. 8.57 days
Answer: 8.57 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(15,20)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/15=4,\quad B=60/20=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{4+3}=8.57
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 20}{15+20}=8.57
\]
Answer: 8.57 days

Q410. A team can finish a job in 12 days of actual work. The work stops for 1 day in between. In how many calendar days will the job be completed?

\]

\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]

Q451. A handles 8 files on his turn and B handles 5 files on the next turn. They continue alternately from A. How many hours are needed for 60 files?

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i

\[

\]

T=9
\]

\[

\]

Q455. 12 workers have done 120 files out of 360. To finish the rest in 10 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 12 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 12 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{240}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 12 workers

Q456. A can do a work in 18 days and B in 24 days. After A works for 4 days alone, both work together. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 9 days
B. 18 days
C. 16 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=20\times 4,\quad R=360-80
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{280}{20+15}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{360-20\times 4}{20+15}
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q457. A alone finishes a job in 24 days, while B alone finishes it in 6 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
A=24/24=1,\quad B=24/6=4
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{1+4}=4.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 6}{24+6}=4.8
\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{204}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 9 workers

Q484. Starting with A, two workers alternate. A completes 10 records and B completes 6 records on their respective turns. How many days will be required to complete 120 records?
A. 14 4/5 days
B. 12 4/5 days
C. 19 4/5 days
D. 17.76 days
Answer: 14 4/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_days_two_workers
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
W_{cycle}=10+6
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=14.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 14 4/5 days

Q487. A takes 8 days to finish a work and B takes 24 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 8 days
B. 6 days
C. 16 days
D. 32 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(8,24)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/8=3,\quad B=24/24=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{3+1}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{8\times 24}{8+24}=6
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q494. If 20 people can use a food stock for 36 days, how long will the stock last for 40 people?
A. 18 days
B. 21 days
C. 21 3/5 days
D. 23 days
Answer: 18 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 36=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 36}{40}
\]
Answer: 18 days

Q497. A team adds 18 metres to a project each day, but 5 metres are lost each day. What is the time for 120 metres?

Answer: 9.23 days

Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work

\]

\[
T=\frac{120}{18-5}
\]

\[
T=9.23
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:

\]
Answer: 9.23 days

Q499. A needs 15 days for a work and A with B needs 8 days. In how many days can B do the work alone?

\]

Q500. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 24 days. After 8 days, the number of people becomes 40. For how many more days will the food last?
A. 15 days
B. 7 days
C. 12 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_remaining_stock
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720-30\times 8}{40}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 12 days
